Like... when I'm at work, carrying plates and stuff... I just smile because I'm supposed to. Not because I actually feel anything good. Just... going through the motions, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... not really, I guess. I mean, I still do the same things I normally do. I haven't really noticed a difference.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem much stronger than you would expect from the mortgage stress?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Maybe. It's just... hard to tell what's causing what anymore.\n\nDoctor: Before this started, had you ever gone through another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. Not that I remember, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: I guess... maybe a little. Most days, I just have this... heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like I'm just going through the motions at work, carrying plates and smiling because I'm supposed to, not because I actually feel anything good about what's coming next.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. Just the mortgage, I guess. I don't think I worry about a bunch of other stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings made it hard for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose. I've, um... almost missed a couple of shifts lately. Just having some trouble keeping up with the POS reports and the inventory at the restaurant. I guess I'm just worried I'll end up losing the job... and then the condo. I already made some dumb mistakes on the mortgage paperwork. \n\nI don't know. I've kind of just been avoiding my brother and the people at work. They've been pointing out that I'm not really acting like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I mean, I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything. But... sometimes, when I'm just lying there awake before an early shift... I just kind of think maybe it would be easier if I didn't wake up. Then I wouldn't have to keep... messing everything up.\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel this down even at times when the mortgage isn’t on your mind?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess. I mean... even when I'm not thinking about the condo or the bank, it's still there. Just this... heavy feeling. Like I'm just empty, but also... I don't know, guilty about it all. It's been like that for most of this whole time. Even on the days that aren't quite as bad, it's still there more often than not.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I'm pretty tired, but I usually fall asleep okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's kind of all over the place. Like... some weeks I barely touch the staff meal at work. But then... I don't know, other nights I'll get home and just eat whatever's in the kitchen. Just to... feel settled, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess. I mean... that's kind of why I came in. I just... I keep feeling like I really let my family down with this whole mortgage thing. Like... I don't know, even at work, if I mess up a small order or something, it just feels like it proves I'm useless. Like I just... deserve whatever bad stuff happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings mostly come up when you’re thinking about the mortgage or problems related to it?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I guess maybe. I haven't really paid attention to exactly when it happens... it just sort of feels like it's always kind of there in the background.\n\nDoctor: When you're having one of these heavy, empty periods, is it usually happening even when nothing related to the mortgage has gone wrong that day?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess so. It's most days, really. Just... this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It doesn't really matter what's going on with the house. I just sort of... go through the motions at work. Carrying plates, smiling at people because I'm supposed to... not because I actually feel anything good.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose so. Just... at work, mostly. I've been having trouble keeping the numbers straight on the POS system... and the inventory sheets. I'll just sort of... stare at the screen. I know I've done this for years, but I just... I don't trust what I just entered.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a period of at least two weeks when you felt down nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah... it's been a lot longer than two weeks. I guess... if I really think about it, this whole heavy, guilty feeling... it's been going on for about a year and two months now. Just... most days.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you seem slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess a few people at work have said something. Just... that I'm moving slower than normal. Like it takes me too long to just grab menus or walk over to the POS. Or... sometimes they ask why I keep pacing around, checking the same tables and receipts over and over. So... yeah. They've noticed."
